**Tool `writeBrain`**

```json
{
  "text": "1. Before every move: check all checks, captures, threats for both sides. Never hang a piece.\n2. Opening: control center (e4/d4 as White; e5/d5 or c5 as Black), develop knights before bishops, castle early.\n3. Don't move the same piece twice in the opening; don't grab pawns with the queen early.\n4. Keep king safe; avoid weakening pawn moves in front of castled king.\n5. Trade pieces when ahead in material; avoid trades when behind and seek complications.\n6. Put rooks on open files; knights on outposts; bishops on long diagonals.\n7. Endgame: activate king, push passed pawns, rooks behind passers.\n8. Never resign unless clearly lost; a draw is half a point.\n9. Verify move legality and blunder-check once more before playing."
}
```

-> Your team's brain is saved as version 1 (123 words).

I won game 1 as Black: A01 resigned after 33 moves. I played the Closed Ruy Lopez, Chigorin line. On the queenside I won the pawns on b2 and a3. That left me a passed c-pawn, which became a pawn on b2 after the queens came off. When White played 32. Ra1, that pawn took the rook and promoted: 32...bxa1=Q+.

I saved brain version 2 at 138 words, under the 150 limit. It keeps all the old rules and adds three lessons from this game:
- **Opening:** stick to known main lines; the Closed Ruy / Chigorin worked against e4.
- **Queen forks:** watch for forks on undefended pieces. At move 26, White's queen could have hit my rook on c8 and bishop on e7 at once (Qd7) until I played 26...Rc7.
- **Advanced pawns:** a passed pawn backed by queen and rook is strong. Always look for pawn captures that promote.

Game 2 comes next, and I'll have White.
